    USA v. Abdul Abdul Kareem (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca9-20-10155 / USCOURTS-ca9-20-10155-0

    FAPD-AI: The Ninth Circuit published an opinion filed November 6, 2026, in United States v. Abdul Malik Abdul Kareem, an appeal from the District of Arizona docketed as Nos. 20-10155, 21-10315, and 24-3202. The panel reversed the district court's judgment on Count 5, a conspiracy to provide material support to ISIS under 18 U.S.C. § 2339B, holding that the government did not present sufficient evidence that Kareem intended to join such a conspiracy, and remanded with instructions to enter a judgment of acquittal on Count 5 and to resentence Kareem. The panel affirmed the district court on all other issues in a separate memorandum disposition, and Judge Collins concurred in part and dissented in part, stating he would uphold the Count 5 conviction. USCOURTS-SEL-01

    Calvin Mayfield v. Ryan Woods, et al (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca7-26-01401 / USCOURTS-ca7-26-01401-0

    FAPD-AI: The Seventh Circuit issued a nonprecedential order in Mayfield v. Woods, No. 26-1401, vacating the district court's screening dismissal under 28 U.S.C. § 1915A of an Illinois prisoner's § 1983 complaint and remanding the case. The court held that the district court erred by not analyzing the First Amendment retaliation and conspiracy claims arising from a disciplinary ticket and a transfer after the plaintiff raised a cell assignment concern. The court also held that the complaint did not allege facts sufficient to state an Equal Protection class-of-one claim against the warden. USCOURTS-SEL-01

    Anderson Ross v. Target (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca5-26-20180 / USCOURTS-ca5-26-20180-0

    FAPD-AI: The Fifth Circuit issued an unpublished per curiam opinion affirming the Southern District of Texas's exclusion of expert witnesses whom plaintiff Erica Anderson Ross designated after the scheduling order deadline in her negligence and premises liability suit against Target Corporation, and affirming the court's subsequent grant of summary judgment to Target. The court applied the four factors used for exclusion under Fed. R. Civ. P. 37(c)(1), finding that the late designations lacked substantive summaries, that Target faced substantial prejudice, and that a continuance would not fully cure it. The court also held that summary judgment was appropriate because Texas law generally requires expert testimony to establish causation of medical conditions outside common knowledge, and Ross conceded that she could not prove causation without such testimony. USCOURTS-SEL-01

    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca5-26-50333 / USCOURTS-ca5-26-50333-0

    FAPD-AI: In consolidated appeals Nos. 26-50332 and 26-50333, United States v. Perdomo-Hernandez, the Fifth Circuit issued an unpublished per curiam order on the Government's motion for summary affirmance. The defendant challenged the constitutionality of the 8 U.S.C. § 1326(b) enhancement for illegal reentry, an argument the court held foreclosed by Almendarez-Torres v. United States and Pervis.     USA v. John Pollard (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca3-26-01860 / USCOURTS-ca3-26-01860-0

    FAPD-AI: The Third Circuit issued a nonprecedential opinion affirming a ten-month sentence imposed on John Courtney Pollard, who pleaded guilty to making interstate threats under 18 U.S.C. § 875. The court held that any miscalculation of the advisory Guidelines range from the district court's refusal to apply the requested four-level reduction under U.S.S.G. § 2A6.1(b)(6)(B) was harmless, because the record showed the court would have imposed the same sentence regardless. The court also rejected Pollard's arguments concerning non-carceral sentencing and the explanation required for the variance. USCOURTS-SEL-01

    USA v. Abdul Abdul Kareem (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca9-21-10315 / USCOURTS-ca9-21-10315-0

    FAPD-AI: On November 6, 2026, the Ninth Circuit issued a published opinion by Judge Mendoza, Jr., reversing the District of Arizona's judgment on Count 5 of Abdul Malik Abdul Kareem's conviction, which charged conspiracy to provide material support to ISIS under 18 U.S.C. § 2339B, and remanding with instructions to enter a judgment of acquittal on that count and to resentence Kareem. The panel held that the government did not present sufficient evidence that Kareem intended to join a conspiracy to provide ISIS personnel or services. Judge Collins concurred in the judgment in part and dissented in part, and the panel affirmed the remaining issues and convictions in a separate memorandum disposition. USCOURTS-SEL-01

    USA v. Perdomo-Hernandez (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca5-26-50332 / USCOURTS-ca5-26-50332-0

    FAPD-AI: On November 6, 2026, the Fifth Circuit issued an unpublished per curiam opinion in consolidated appeals Nos. 26-50332 and 26-50333, affirming Cristhian Perdomo-Hernandez's conviction and sentence for illegal reentry and the revocation of his supervised release in a separate case. Perdomo-Hernandez challenged the enhancement in 8 U.S.C. § 1326(b) as unconstitutional, did not challenge the revocation judgment, and acknowledged the argument is foreclosed by Almendarez-Torres v. United States and United States v. Pervis. The court granted the government's motion for summary affirmance, denied its alternative motion for an extension of time, and affirmed the district court's judgments. USCOURTS-SEL-01

  • Observed at 15:32:21 Eastern time ET
    USA v. Gerald Cook, Jr. (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca6-25-03657 / USCOURTS-ca6-25-03657-0

    FAPD-AI: On November 6, 2026, the Sixth Circuit issued a published opinion by Judge Thapar, joined by Judge Hermandorfer, affirming the judgment of the Northern District of Ohio against Gerald M. Cook, Jr., who pled guilty to gun and drug charges and was sentenced to 248 months of imprisonment. Cook appealed the denial of his request for a Franks hearing on the warrant affidavit for the search of his home, as well as the reasonableness of his below-Guidelines sentence. The court held that Cook did not make a substantial preliminary showing of an intentional or reckless falsehood in the affidavit, that courts may consider reliable evidence outside the affidavit, including police reports, in making that determination, and that his alternative ineffective-assistance claim was premature. Judge Moore filed a separate opinion concurring in part and concurring in the judgment. USCOURTS-SEL-01

  • Observed at 15:32:20 Eastern time ET
    US v. DaQuan Lane (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca4-24-04312 / USCOURTS-ca4-24-04312-0

    FAPD-AI: In an unpublished per curiam opinion filed November 6, 2026, the Fourth Circuit affirmed the Eastern District of Virginia's denial of Daquan Lane's motion to suppress evidence seized from his vehicle, following his conditional guilty plea to drug distribution, firearm, and felon-in-possession offenses and his 78-month sentence. The court held that officers had reasonable suspicion for the traffic stop based on a perceived child-restraint violation, that Officer Woods had probable cause to open the car door, and that the arrest and removal of Lane were reasonable. The court therefore affirmed the district court's judgment. USCOURTS-SEL-01

  • Observed at 15:32:20 Eastern time ET
    US v. Mark Crenshaw (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca4-25-06173 / USCOURTS-ca4-25-06173-0

    FAPD-AI: In an unpublished per curiam opinion filed November 6, 2026, the Fourth Circuit affirmed the Eastern District of Virginia's order granting in part Mark Kerry Crenshaw's motion for compassionate release under 18 U.S.C. § 3582(c)(1)(A). The court granted Crenshaw's motion to supplement the issues on appeal, considered those issues, and found no reversible error in the district court's decision not to grant a larger sentence reduction. The court cited Rutherford v. United States and Greenlaw v. United States in affirming the order. USCOURTS-SEL-01

    Sarahia Benn v. Brian Tucci (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca4-26-02000 / USCOURTS-ca4-26-02000-0

    FAPD-AI: In an unpublished per curiam opinion filed November 6, 2026, the Fourth Circuit dismissed Sarahia Benn's appeals, Nos. 26-2000 and 26-2003, from orders of the District of Maryland dismissing her bankruptcy appeals and denying reconsideration. The court held that the underlying bankruptcy court orders were not final under 28 U.S.C. § 158(a)(1) and that it therefore lacked jurisdiction under 28 U.S.C. § 158(d)(1). The appeals were dismissed for lack of jurisdiction. USCOURTS-SEL-01

  • Observed at 15:32:20 Eastern time ET
    USA v. Asberry (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca5-25-11337 / USCOURTS-ca5-25-11337-0

    FAPD-AI: In an unpublished per curiam opinion filed November 6, 2026, the Fifth Circuit affirmed Fredrick Asberry's conviction on one count of conspiracy to distribute controlled substances and four counts of possession with intent to distribute, and his 222-month sentence. The court held that Asberry was present when trial began under Federal Rule of Criminal Procedure 43, so his later removal for disruptive conduct did not constitute plain error, and that the Second Superseding Indictment's misattributed reference in the jury charge did not constitute a constructive amendment. The court also held that the evidence, including text messages and drug packaging, was sufficient to support the jury's finding of an agreement. USCOURTS-SEL-01

  • Observed at 15:32:20 Eastern time ET
    Alcala v. Bosco Auto Group (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca5-25-40213 / USCOURTS-ca5-25-40213-0

    FAPD-AI: In an unpublished per curiam opinion filed November 6, 2026, the Fifth Circuit affirmed the Eastern District of Texas's Rule 12(b)(6) dismissal with prejudice of Virginia Alcala's Truth in Lending Act claims against Bosco Auto Group, a dismissal entered by a magistrate judge acting by consent. The court found no abuse of discretion in consolidating the claims with those against Unity One Credit Union, and held that the statutory text of 15 U.S.C. § 1635(a) did not support her rescission argument. The court also rejected her Seventh Amendment, due process, bias, and post-judgment motion arguments. USCOURTS-SEL-01

    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca5-26-10298 / USCOURTS-ca5-26-10298-0

    FAPD-AI: In an unpublished per curiam opinion filed November 6, 2026, the Fifth Circuit granted the government's unopposed motion for summary affirmance of the Northern District of Texas judgment revoking Lucas Monrajas's supervised release and imposing 10 months of imprisonment. Monrajas had argued for the first time on appeal that 18 U.S.C. § 3583(g) is unconstitutional under United States v. Haymond, and he conceded that the argument is foreclosed by United States v. Garner. The court denied the government's alternative motion for an extension of time to file a brief and affirmed the district court's judgment. USCOURTS-SEL-01

    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca5-26-10308 / USCOURTS-ca5-26-10308-0

    FAPD-AI: On November 6, 2026, the Fifth Circuit issued an unpublished per curiam opinion affirming a district court judgment that revoked Montavion Jones's supervised release, imposed 12 months of imprisonment, and imposed two years of supervised release. Jones argued for the first time on appeal that 18 U.S.C. § 3583(g) is unconstitutional under United States v. Haymond and that he was denied a jury determination of whether he committed a crime, and he conceded both arguments are foreclosed by circuit precedent.     Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca5-26-50208 / USCOURTS-ca5-26-50208-0

    FAPD-AI: On November 6, 2026, the Fifth Circuit issued an unpublished per curiam opinion affirming the conviction and sentence of Agustin Cano-Quintero for illegal reentry under 8 U.S.C. § 1326(a) and (b). Cano-Quintero argued for the first time on appeal that the recidivism enhancement in § 1326(b) is unconstitutional, and the court found that argument foreclosed by Almendarez-Torres v. United States.     Ricardo McClinton, et al v. Warden, Baldwin State Prison, et al (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca11-25-10654
    2 documents in this case
    • Observed at 15:32:19 Eastern time ET
      Ricardo McClinton, et al v. Warden, Baldwin State Prison, et al (opens in a new tab)
      Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca11-25-10654 / USCOURTS-ca11-25-10654-0

      FAPD-AI: In this opinion dated April 14, 2026, the U.S. Court of Appeals for the Eleventh Circuit affirmed summary judgment for the Georgia Department of Corrections officials sued in the wrongful death and estate Eighth Amendment failure-to-protect action arising from the 2021 stabbing death of Jamari McClinton at Baldwin State Prison. The court held that none of the defendants had the subjective knowledge of the risk of harm to McClinton required to overcome qualified immunity. The opinion also stated that the court did not reach the statute of limitations issues raised as to the parents' time-barred claims. USCOURTS-SEL-01

    • Observed at 15:32:19 Eastern time ET
      Ricardo McClinton, et al v. Warden, Baldwin State Prison, et al (opens in a new tab)
      Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca11-25-10654 / USCOURTS-ca11-25-10654-1

      FAPD-AI: In a revised opinion filed November 6, 2026, the U.S. Court of Appeals for the Eleventh Circuit sua sponte vacated its prior opinion in McClinton v. Warden, Baldwin State Prison and substituted the revised opinion. The revised opinion states that the only change is the deletion of former footnote 6. It affirms summary judgment for the defendants on qualified immunity, holding that none of them possessed the subjective knowledge of the risk of harm to McClinton required for an Eighth Amendment deliberate indifference claim. USCOURTS-SEL-01

  • Observed at 15:32:19 Eastern time ET
    Roy Chambers, Jr. v. Brooks Benton, et al (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca11-25-13975 / USCOURTS-ca11-25-13975-0

    FAPD-AI: In this unpublished per curiam opinion, the U.S. Court of Appeals for the Eleventh Circuit affirmed summary judgment on qualified immunity for prison officials in Roy Chambers, Jr.'s 42 U.S.C. § 1983 Eighth Amendment conditions-of-confinement and deliberate indifference claims. The claims arose from an April 17, 2020 fire in a dormitory at Coastal State Prison and alleged smoke inhalation injuries. The court held that Warden Benton and Officer Mack did not act with subjective recklessness, because the record showed they knew the fire safety systems were inoperable and responded with measures including 30-minute fire checks. USCOURTS-SEL-01

  • Observed at 15:32:19 Eastern time ET
    USA v. Jabin Bethea (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca11-26-11148 / USCOURTS-ca11-26-11148-0

    FAPD-AI: In this unpublished order, the U.S. Court of Appeals for the Eleventh Circuit granted the government's motion to dismiss the appeal of Jabin Bethea, who pleaded guilty to one count of depriving an individual of constitutional rights under color of state law in violation of 18 U.S.C. § 242 and received a 20-month sentence. Bethea had challenged the application of a Sentencing Guidelines obstruction of justice enhancement and the substantive reasonableness of his sentence. The court held that the appeal waiver in his plea agreement was knowing and voluntary, that no exception to the waiver applied, and that enforcing it would not result in a miscarriage of justice. USCOURTS-SEL-01

  • Observed at 15:32:19 Eastern time ET
    Cullerton v. US (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca13-26-01633 / USCOURTS-ca13-26-01633-0

    FAPD-AI: In this nonprecedential per curiam decision dated November 6, 2026, the U.S. Court of Appeals for the Federal Circuit affirmed the Court of Federal Claims' dismissal of Thomas Kevin Cullerton's complaint seeking $95,000 in severance pay after his removal from the U.S. Forest Service. The court held that Cullerton was immediately eligible for a minimum retirement age plus 10 years of service (MRA+10) annuity under 5 U.S.C. § 8412(g)(1), which disqualifies an employee from severance pay under 5 U.S.C. § 5595(a)(2)(iv) and 5 C.F.R. § 550.704(b)(5). The court further held that his decision not to apply for that annuity and the alleged failure of the USDA to forward his records to OPM did not alter that eligibility. USCOURTS-SEL-01

    Wallace Collier v. Woodrow Myers (opens in a new tab)
    Judicial opinion · govinfo API · USCOURTS-ca11-22-11188 / USCOURTS-ca11-22-11188-0

    FAPD-AI: In this unpublished per curiam opinion, the U.S. Court of Appeals for the Eleventh Circuit affirmed summary judgment for Woodrow Myers, former Chief Operating Officer of Corizon Health, Inc., in a 42 U.S.C. § 1983 suit by Florida prisoner Wallace Gary Collier. Collier alleged that a delay in treating his hepatitis C caused liver fibrosis, and the court held that he could not establish causation because the record lacked evidence of when his fibrosis began or progressed. The opinion also denied Collier's request for judicial notice of expert testimony from a separate class action, because he sought to use it for the truth of the matters asserted. USCOURTS-SEL-01
